\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

The top horizontal arrow is a quasi-equivalence constructed by Gammage–Shende
[
GS22
]
and the bottom horizontal arrow is a quasi-equivalence constructed by Abouzaid
[
Abo06
,
Abo09
]
.
On the lower right-hand side, we have the “monomially admissible Fukaya categories”:
\mathcal{F}_{mon}
is the category of tropical Lagrangian sections of
S
;
\partial\mathcal{F}_{mon}
is the subcategory of the compact Fukaya category
\mathcal{F}(F)
consisting of boundaries of tropical Lagrangian sections. On the left-hand side,
\Pic_{dg}(Y^{*})
is the category of line bundles on
Y^{*}
, and
\Pic_{dg}(\partial Y^{*})
is the category of line bundles on
\partial Y^{*}
that are restrictions of line bundles on
Y^{*}
. On both sides, the
M^{*}
superscript indicates that these categories are
M^{*}
-graded. The functors
i^{!}
,
A
, and
\cap_{mon}
are
M^{*}
-graded; we show that the middle horizontal arrow can also be made
M^{*}
-graded, on the subcategory we are interested in, in
Section

5
.

4.1
Homological mirror symmetry for Fano toric varieties

We begin with homological mirror symmetry where the toric variety
Y^{*}
is on the
B
-side. The relevant dg category associated with
Y^{*}
is
D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})
.
On the
A
-side, the relevant
A_{\infty}
category is the wrapped Fukaya category
\mathcal{W}(S)
of
S
from
Section

3.1
. We equip cotangent fibers (and Lagrangians Hamiltonian isotopic to them) and the zero section
M^{*}_{S^{1}}
with canonical grading/orientation data coming from the tautological polarization of
T^{*}M^{*}_{S^{1}}\simeq M^{*}_{\C^{*}}
(see
Section

3.1
).

We will recall two independently established statements of homological mirror symmetry for toric varieties from the literature relating
\mathcal{W}(S)
and
D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})
. We will then prove a few properties of these quasi-equivalences and use a theorem of Bondal and Orlov
[
BO01
]
to show that they coincide for smooth Fano toric varieties.

We will use a slight enlargement of the category
\mathcal{W}(S)
.
Namely, the objects will be pairs
(L,\zeta)
where
L
is an exact and cylindrical Lagrangian brane and
\zeta
is a
\Bbbk
-local system on
L
; counts of holomorphic discs are weighted by the holonomy of the local systems around the boundary. In fact, we will only need to consider local systems on the exact Lagrangian torus
M^{*}_{S^{1}}
. Note that the
\Bbbk
-local systems on
S

are in natural correspondence with
M_{\Bbbk^{*}}
from the isomorphisms
\pi_{1}(S)\simeq\pi_{1}(M^{*}_{S^{1}})\simeq M^{*}
.

{rmk}

Allowing Lagrangian objects to be equipped with local systems does not change any of the properties that we will use about
\mathcal{W}(S)
essentially due to the fact that
\mathcal{W}(S)
is generated by simply connected Lagrangians. In particular, the main tools of
[
GPS24a
]
such as the Künneth embedding, wrapping exact triangle, and stop removal are still valid.

One powerful approach to proving homological mirror symmetry for toric varieties is to pass through a microlocal sheaf category.
This method has given the most general proofs, and for us, has the important benefit of having been proven to be compatible with restriction to the toric boundary (see
Section

4.2
).
There is a long history of computing microlocal sheaf categories corresponding to derived categories of toric varieties beginning with the announcement
[
Bon06
]
and results of
[
Fan+11
,
Fan+12
]
, and culminating in the general proof of
[
Kuw20
]
.
In these works, the category of interest is the dg category
\mathrm{Sh}_{-\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}}(M^{*}_{S^{1}})^{c}
of compact objects in the category
\mathrm{Sh}_{-\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}}(M^{*}_{S^{1}})
of sheaves of dg modules on
M^{*}_{S^{1}}
with microsupport in
-\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}
, where the negation denotes applying multiplication by
-1
only on the
M_{\R}
factor of
M^{*}_{S^{1}}\times M_{\R}=T^{*}M^{*}_{S^{1}}\simeq M^{*}_{\C^{*}}
. When combined with the general correspondence
[
GPS24
]
between microlocal sheaf categories and partially wrapped Fukaya categories, we collectively obtain the following (after restricting to our setting):

Theorem 20

(
[
Kuw20
,
GPS24
]
)
.

There are quasi-equivalences of
A_{\infty}
categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{-\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}}(M^{*}_{S^{1}})^{c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})\simeq\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KGPS}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GPS}

{rmk}

By
[
Kuw20
, Remark 9.3]
, the functor
K
agrees with the non-equivariant coherent-constructible correspondence in
[
Tre10
,
Fan+12
]
.

The equivalence
GPS
holds more generally for cotangent bundles of analytic manifolds. We will use the following property of this equivalence.

Lemma 21
.

Let
Q
be an oriented real analytic manifold and let
\Lambda\subset S^{*}Q
be a closed subanalytic isotropic subset. The image of a local system
\zeta
on
Q
under the quasi-equivalence

\mathrm{Sh}_{-\Lambda}(Q)^{c}\xrightarrow{GPS}\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(T^{*}Q,\Lambda)

from
[
GPS24
, Theorem 1.1]
is quasi-isomorphic to
(Q,\zeta)
.

Proof 4.1
.

The quasi-equivalence
GPS
is constructed by refining
\Lambda
to be the conormal to a Whitney triangulation
\mathcal{S}
. We can assume we are in that setting as all linking disks are orthogonal to
(Q,\xi)
and the stop removal functor is the quotient by linking disks by
[
GPS24a
, Theorem 1.20]
.

In that case,
GPS
passes through an intermediate quasi-equivalence with perfect complexes over
\mathcal{S}
. The proof of
[
GPS24
, Theorem 5.36]
shows that
(Q,\Bbbk_{Q})
is identified with the indicator module
1_{Q}
in
\text{Perf }\mathcal{S}
where
\Bbbk_{Q}
is the trivial local system. The sheaf
\Bbbk_{Q}
is also identified with
1_{Q}
by
[
GPS24
, Lemmas 4.6 and 4.7]
. For general local systems, the same calculations apply to show that the local system
HF^{*}((Q,\zeta),L_{q})
for
q\in Q
defined using continuation elements as in
[
GPS24
, Lemma 5.10]
coincides with
\zeta
.

Now, we prove two properties of
KGPS
. We let
[0]\in M_{S^{1}}^{*}
be the additive identity and set
L_{[0]}
to be the inward conormal to a small ball around
[0]
.
6
6

6

Similar Lagrangians are considered in
[
GPS24
, Section 5.7]
. However, we take the opposite convention as
L_{s}
in
[
GPS24
]
denotes the outward conormal to a small ball around
s
.

Proposition 22
.

The image of the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{*}}
under
KGPS
is quasi-isomorphic to
L_{[0]}
, and for all
\xi\in M_{\Bbbk^{*}}
, the image of the shifted skyscraper sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\xi}[-n]
is quasi-isomorphic to
(M^{*}_{S^{1}},\xi)
.

Proof 4.2
.

For the structure sheaf,
[
Kuw20
, Lemma 10.1]
shows that
K
sends
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{*}}
to the sheaf
\Bbbk_{[0]}
where
[0]\in M_{S^{1}}^{*}
is the unit. In particular, this object co-represents the stalk functor at
[0]
. On the other hand, the calculations in
[
GPS24
, Section 5.7]
imply that the image of
L_{[0]}
under
GPS^{-1}
co-represents the stalk functor at
[0]
when
\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}
is refined to be the conormal to a Whitney triangulation. However, this property persists under stop removal as
L_{[0]}
is left-orthogonal to the linking disks of the removed strata.

For
\xi\in M_{\Bbbk^{*}}
,
[
TWZ19
, Section 2]
shows that
K
takes
\mathcal{O}_{\xi}[-n]
to
\xi
. We can then apply
Lemma

21
to deduce the result.

A second approach to homological mirror symmetry for toric varieties is to stay within the realm of Floer theory. This route will be beneficial to us for its direct access to Lagrangian submanifolds as objects. Following
[
Abo06
,
Abo09
,
Han19
]
, we consider
A
- and
B
-side categories whose objects are integral piecewise-linear functions
\mathbf{F}:M_{\R}\to\R
which are linear on each cone of
\Sigma^{*}
.

On the
A
-side, one constructs a Lagrangian submanifold
\tilde{L}_{mon}(\mathbf{F})\subset T^{*}M^{*}_{S^{1}}\simeq M^{*}_{\C^{*}}
corresponding to each such
\mathbf{F}
, which comes equipped with a canonical grading as well as an ‘anchoring’ in the sense of
[
Fuk+10
]
, i.e., a lift to the universal cover
T^{*}M^{*}_{\R}\to T^{*}M^{*}_{S^{1}}
.
The anchoring of
\tilde{L}_{mon}(\mathbf{F})
is the graph of the differential of a smoothing of
\mathbf{F}
, under the identification
T^{*}M^{*}_{\R}=M^{*}_{\R}\times M_{\R}=T^{*}M_{\R}
(see
Definition

35
for more details).
As a result, the Lagrangians
\tilde{L}_{mon}(\mathbf{F})
are sections of
\pi
(and hence also
\Log
) and are referred to as tropical Lagrangian sections.
The category of such tropical Lagrangians sections is denoted
\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}
, and comes equipped with a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-grading (the
\Z
-grading is the cohomological grading, and the
M^{*}
-grading comes from the anchorings).

On the
B
-side, one considers the equivariant line bundles
\mathcal{O}(\mathbf{F})
corresponding to support functions.
The category of such equivariant line bundles is denoted
\Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(Y^{*})
, and comes equipped with a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-grading (the
\Z
-grading is the cohomological grading, and the
M^{*}
-grading is given by characters of the algebraic torus whose action on the morphism spaces is determined by the equivariant structures).
In
[
Abo09
]
, Abouzaid constructs a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded
A_{\infty}
quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{A}\Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(Y^{*}),\qquad\text{which identifies}

\displaystyle\tilde{L}_{mon}(\mathbf{F})

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y^{*}}(\mathbf{F}).

In
[
HH22
, Theorem A]
, a
\Z
-graded fully faithful embedding

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}

\displaystyle\xhookrightarrow{HH^{pre}}\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})\qquad\text{sending}

\displaystyle\tilde{L}_{mon}(\mathbf{F})

\displaystyle\mapsto\tilde{L}_{cyl}(\mathbf{F})

(which forgets the
M^{*}
-grading) is constructed, and its image is shown to generate in
[
HH22
, Corollary C]
.
Denote the composition with the quasi-inverse of the quasi-equivalence
\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)\to\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})
by
HH:\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}\to\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)
.
Combining that result with the fact that
D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})
is generated by line bundles
[
Abo09
, Proposition 1.3]
, one obtains the following when restricting to our setting:

Theorem 23

(
[
Abo09
,
HH22
]
)
.

There is a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HH}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces AHH}

which commutes up to quasi-isomorphism of
A_{\infty}
functors.
The top functor is a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded quasi-isomorphism, the vertical functors are
\Z
-graded and cohomologically fully faithful, and the bottom functor is a
\Z
-graded quasi-equivalence.

We now check that
AHH
satisfies the analogue of
Proposition

22
.

Proposition 24
.

The image of the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{*}}
under
AHH
is quasi-isomorphic to
L_{[0]}
, and for all
\xi\in M_{\Bbbk^{*}}
, the image of the shifted skyscraper sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\xi}[-n]
under
AHH
is quasi-isomorphic to
(M^{*}_{S^{1}},\xi)
.

Proof 4.3
.

By definition,
AHH
takes
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{*}}
to
\tilde{L}_{cyl}(0)
. However,
\tilde{L}_{cyl}(0)
is Hamiltonian isotopic to
L_{[0]}
in the complement of
\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}}
via a linear isotopy of their primitives using the argument in
[
HH22
, Lemma 3.14]
.

We now consider the stop removal functor
\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})\to\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}})
which is intertwined with the restriction
D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})\to D^{b}_{dg}Coh(M_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})
under
AHH
by
[
HH22
, Proposition 6.8]
. Moreover, the stop removal functor is the quotient by linking disks by
[
GPS24a
, Theorem 1.20]
, and the linking disks are orthogonal to
(M^{*}_{S^{1}},\xi)
. In addition,
AHH
is compatible with taking products. Thus, we are reduced to checking the claim for the quasi-equivalence between
D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\mathbb{G}_{m})
and
\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(T^{*}S^{1})
which identifies
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
with the cotangent fiber
L_{[0]}
.

In that case, we have
HW(L_{[0]},L_{[0]})\simeq\Bbbk[z^{\pm}]
respecting product structures and concentrated in degree zero. In fact, we can choose a wrapping of
L_{[0]}
so that the powers of
z
are in bijection with intersection points and this isomorphism holds on the chain level. We therefore need to calculate
HW(L_{[0]},(T,\xi))\simeq\Bbbk\langle x\rangle
as a left
\Bbbk[z^{\pm}]
-module where
x
is a formal basis element of degree
n
. That is, we need to compute
\mu_{2}(z,x)
as a multiple of
x
. There is exactly one disc on the cylinder contributing to this product and the portion of its boundary on
S^{1}
sweeps out the circle once positively. Moreover, with the chosen orientation data on
S^{1}
, this disk contributes positively, and we obtain
\mu_{2}(z,x)=\xi x
as desired.

The proof of Proposition
27
will make use of some facts concerning autoequivalences of
D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*}).
We first record the following preliminary technical result concerning Fourier-Mukai functors.

Lemma 25
.

[
CS12
, Theorem 1.2]
Let
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
be two smooth projective varieties and let

\displaystyle G:H^{0}(D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y_{1}))\to H^{0}(D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y_{2}))

be an exact functor between triangulated categories. If
G
is a Fourier-Mukai functor, meaning
G\cong\phi_{\mathcal{E}}
for some
\mathcal{E}\in D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y_{1}\times Y_{2})
, then the cohomology sheaves of
\mathcal{E}
are uniquely determined (up to isomorphism) by
G
.

We apply this result to obtain the following dg-lift of a celebrated result of Bondal and Orlov:

Theorem 26
.

Let
Y^{*}
be any smooth Fano variety. The autoequivalences of
D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})
are generated by automorphisms of
Y^{*}
, twists by line bundles, and the shift functor. In other words, we have

\displaystyle Aut(D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*}))\cong Aut(Y^{*})\ltimes(Pic(Y^{*})\oplus\Z)

where
\Z
is generated by the shift functor.

Proof 4.4
.

In
[
BO01
, Theorem 3.1]
, Bondal and Orlov prove this result at the level of triangulated categories. To enhance this to the dg-level, we note that the Fourier-Mukai kernels
\mathcal{E}
corresponding to each of these autoequivalences are cohomologically concentrated in a single degree. They thus lift uniquely (up to quasi-isomorphism) to
D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*}\times Y^{*})
.

Proposition 27
.

The quasi-equivalences
KGPS
and
AHH
are quasi-isomorphic. In particular,
KGPS
sends
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{*}}(\mathbf{F})\mapsto\tilde{L}_{cyl}(\mathbf{F})
for every toric divisor on
Y^{*}
.

Proof 4.5
.

By Theorem
26
,
G=KGPS^{-1}\circ AHH
determines an element of
Aut(Y^{*})\ltimes(Pic(Y^{*})\oplus\Z).
By the first parts of
Proposition

22
and
Proposition

24
,
G
preserves the isomorphism class of the structure sheaf. Thus,
G
is induced by an automorphism
f
of
Y^{*}
.

Begin by assuming that
\Bbbk
is algebraically closed. Then, we can identify this automorphism by its action on closed points. The second parts of
Proposition

22
and
Proposition

24
show that
f
preserves every point in the dense torus orbit of
Y^{*}
. Thus,
f=id
and hence
G
is quasi-isomorphic to the identity.

For general fields, we base change to the algebraic closure
\overline{\Bbbk}
. Then because both
AHH
and
KGPS
are compatible with base-change, the autoequivalence
G
is quasi-isomorphic to the identity and the automorphism
f
becomes the identity after base change to
\overline{\Bbbk}
. To conclude, note that
f
is induced by a map on homogeneous coordinate rings which becomes the identity after tensoring with
\overline{\Bbbk}
. This implies that
f
is already the identity.

{rmk}

A careful reader will note that
[
Abo09
,
HH22
,
Kuw20
]
are all written with
\Bbbk=\C
for simplicity. However, their arguments work with no modifications over any other coefficient field. For
[
Kuw20
]
, this has been previously claimed in
[
GS22
,
TWZ19
]
.

4.2
Homological mirror symmetry for the affine hypersurface

We now discuss homological mirror symmetry with the toric boundary
\partial Y^{*}
on the
B
-side and
F
on the
A
-side, where
F
is the completion of the Liouvile domain
F_{0}\subset\partial B
from
Section

2.4
. The relevant categories are
D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*})
and
\mathcal{W}(F)
(see
Section

3.1
).

Homological mirror symmetry in this setting was proved by Gammage and Shende
[
GS22
]
using
[
GPS24
]
to convert sheaf-theoretic calculations to Fukaya categories and
[
Zho20
]
to calculate skeleta.
In line with (
2.4
), we let
-\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}}
be the boundary at infinity of
-\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}
.
The microlocal sheaf analog of
\mathcal{W}(F)
is the dg category
\mu\mathrm{sh}_{-\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}}}(-\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})^{c}
of compact objects in the dg category of microlocal sheaves on
-\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}}
(see
[
GPS24
, Section 7.5]
for a complete definition).
We let
\mu^{*}:\mu\mathrm{sh}_{-\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}}}(-\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})^{c}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{-\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}}(M^{*}_{S^{1}})^{c}
be the left adjoint to microlocalization.
Then,
[
Zho20
]
’s computation of the skeleton of
F
suffices to compute the cup functor in terms of these microlocal sheaf categories using the following result of
[
GPS24
]
:

Theorem 28

(Cf. Equations (1.4) and (7.31) of
[
GPS24
]
)
.

There is a diagram of
A_{\infty}
functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\mathrm{sh}_{-\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}}}(-\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})^{c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{-\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}}(M^{*}_{S^{1}})^{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GPS}

in which the horizontal functors are quasi-equivalences, and the diagram commutes up to natural quasi-isomorphism.

Then,
[
GS22
]
compare these microlocal sheaf categories with coherent sheaves:

Theorem 29

(
[
GS22
]
, Theorem 7.4.1)
.

There is a diagram of
A_{\infty}
functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\mathrm{sh}_{-\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}}}(-\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})^{c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{-\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}}(M^{*}_{S^{1}})^{c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}

in which the horizontal functors are quasi-equivalences, and the diagram commutes up to natural quasi-isomorphism.

Putting
28
and
29
together and using
Corollary

18
to trade
\mathcal{W}(F)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cup}}{{\to}}\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})
for
\mathcal{W}(F)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cup}}{{\to}}\mathcal{W}(S)
, we obtain homological mirror symmetry for the affine hypersurface intertwining
\cup
and
i_{*}
:

Theorem 30

(
[
GS22
]
)
.

There is a diagram of
A_{\infty}
functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GS}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KGPS}

in which the horizontal functors are quasi-equivalences, and the diagram commutes up to natural quasi-isomorphism.

Before proceeding further, we need to discuss the shriek pull-back functor
i^{!}
. Note that there is a formal dg-adjoint (see Appendix
A.1
) to
i_{*}
:

\displaystyle(i_{*})^{*}:\operatorname{Perf}D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})\to\operatorname{Mod}D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*}).

Because
D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})
is already triangulated and split-closed, we may remove ‘
\operatorname{Perf}
’ from the source.
Observe that by the adjunction between
H(i_{*})
and (derived) shriek pull-back, we have that this adjoint functor agrees with the shriek pull-back functor on the level of cohomology, and in particular lands in
\operatorname{Perf}D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*})=D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*})
. We take
i^{!}
to be the resulting functor:

\displaystyle i^{!}:D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})\to D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*}).

{rmk}

Because dg-adjoints are unique up to quasi-isomorphism of functors (see Appendix
A.1
), the above definition of
i^{!}
will agree (up to quasi-isomorphism) with any other definition of shriek pull-back that satisfies a dg-adjunction with
i_{*}
.

Corollary 31
.

There is a diagram of
A_{\infty}
functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GS}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KGPS}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup^{*}}

which commutes up to natural quasi-isomorphism, and where
\cup^{*}
is a right-adjoint to
\cup
.

Proof 4.6
.

By Theorem
30
and the naturality of taking the pullback of modules, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Mod}\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GS}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KGPS}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup^{*}}

(4.1)

Because
\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)
is triangulated and split-closed, we may remove the outer ‘
\operatorname{Perf}
’ from the bottom row. It follows by commutativity of the diagram that
\cup^{*}
also lands in
\operatorname{Perf}\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)=\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)
, which yields the claimed commutative diagram.

4.3
The cap functor

We need a geometric understanding of the equivalence
GS
.
In this subsection, we will show that the restriction of a line bundle to
\partial Y^{*}
is mirror to a compact Lagrangian.
We do this by calculating the adjoint
\cap
to
\cup
on the subcategory of Lagrangians mirror to line bundles. This
\cap
functor will be
\mathbb{Z}\oplus M^{*}
graded.
The results of this subsection invoke one technical result,
Proposition

34
, which we temporarily defer to
Section

4.4
.

Proposition 32
.

There exists a diagram of
A_{\infty}
categories and functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HH}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap_{mon}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup^{*}}

(4.2)

which commutes up to natural quasi-isomorphism. Furthermore:

1.

The top and bottom arrows are injective on objects and homotopy equivalences on morphisms (in particular, they are cohomologically fully faithful);

2.

\cap_{mon}
is
\mathbb{Z}\oplus M^{*}
graded.

.

The desired diagram follows from commutativity of the outer face of the following diagram:

\partial\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}
\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}

\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)

\cap^{M^{*}}

HH_{P}

to be the full subcategory whose objects lie in the image of

where

is as in

. We thus obtain a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded functor
\cap_{mon}:\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}\to\partial\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}
which makes the upper left square of the following diagram commute.

Let
\Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(\partial Y^{*})
be the full subcategory on objects in the image of
\Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(Y^{*})\to D^{b}_{dg}Coh(Y^{*})\to D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*})
.

Proposition 33
.

There is a diagram of
A_{\infty}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(\partial Y^{*})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}},}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap_{mon}}

in which

-graded;

the top and bottom functors are quasi-isomorphisms;

the top arrow of the diagram fits into a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*})}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GS}

which commutes up to natural quasi-isomorphism.

Proof 4.8
.

The bottom functor is constructed and shown to be a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded quasi-isomorphism in
Theorem

23
.
The left vertical functor is manifestly
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded; the right vertical functor is constructed, and shown to be
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded, in
Proposition

32
.

In order to construct the top functor, we observe that both desired commutative diagrams fit in as the left and top faces of the following cube, where every map apart from the dashed line has been defined, and
j_{A},j_{B},\tilde{j}_{B}
are inclusions of subcategories.

Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(\partial Y^{*})
Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(Y^{*})
\partial\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}
\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}
\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)
\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(F)

D^{b}_{dg}Coh(\partial Y^{*})
i^{!}
\cap_{mon}

j_{B}

Theorem

23
and
Proposition

Corollary

Proposition

(4.3)

The outer face commutes by definition of the category
\Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(\partial Y^{*})
.
The bottom, inner, and right faces commute up to natural quasi-isomorphism by
23
and
27
,
Proposition

32
, and
Corollary

31
respectively.
We construct the dashed arrow so that the top face of the commutative diagram commutes up to natural quasi-isomorphism, by applying
Lemma

85
to the functors
\mathcal{F}=GS\circ j_{B}
and
\mathcal{G}=j_{A}
.
To check the hypotheses of the Lemma, we first observe that both
j_{A}
and
GS\circ j_{B}
are cohomologically fully faithful, as
j_{A}
and
j_{B}
are inclusions of subcategories and
GS
is a quasi-equivalence.
To check that
GS\circ j_{B}
and
j_{A}
have the same essential image, we use the commutativity of the bottom, inner, right, and outer faces, to show that
j_{A}\circ\cap_{mon}\circ A
is naturally isomorphic to
GS\circ j_{B}\circ i^{!}
; together with the fact that
A
,
\cap_{mon}
, and the left-hand version of
i^{!}
are essentially surjective by definition.
Thus we may construct the dashed arrow, and conclude that it is a quasi-equivalence, by
Lemma

85
.
The commutativity of the left-hand and top square then follows from the commutativity of the remaining squares.

4.4
Arclike Lagrangians mirror to line bundles on toric varieties

In §
4.4.1
, we recall the construction of the tropical Lagrangian section
\tilde{L}_{mon}(\mathbf{F})
from
[
Han19
]
. We prove it is arclike, and define
\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})
to be its negative pushoff in
S
in the sense of Appendix
A
. In §
4.4.2
, we recall the construction of the cylindrical (but not arclike) tropical Lagrangian section
\tilde{L}_{cyl}(\mathbf{F})
from
[
HH22
]
. Then, in §
4.4.3
, we prove
Proposition

40
, which says that the Lagrangians
\tilde{L}_{cyl}(\mathbf{F})
and
\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})
(together with their canonical anchorings) define quasi-isomorphic objects of
\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})^{M^{*}}
. Using this, we easily deduce the following result, which is the main result of this section, and a necessary input for the last subsection’s
Proposition

32
.

Proof 4.9
.

We apply
Lemma

85
to the
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded versions of the functors
\mathcal{F}=HH
and
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{Y}_{(-)}
.
These are both cohomologically fully faithful.

By
Proposition

40
, there exists a quasi-isomorphism in
\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})^{M^{*}}
between the Lagrangian submanifolds
\tilde{L}_{cyl}(\mathbf{F})
and
\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})
. By pulling back this isomorphism along the inclusion (i.e., by applying the quasi-inverse of the inclusion quasi-equivalence)
(i^{\prime})^{*}:\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})^{M^{*}}\to\operatorname{Perf}\mathcal{W}(S)^{M^{*}}
, we obtain quasi-isomorphisms
HH(\tilde{L}_{mon}(\mathbf{F}))=(i^{\prime})^{*}\tilde{L}_{cyl}(\mathbf{F})\simeq\mathcal{Y}_{\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})}
.
Thus the essential image of
HH
is contained in that of
\mathcal{Y}_{(-)}
, so the result follows by
Lemma

85
.

4.4.1
Arclike tropical Lagrangian sections:
\tilde{L}_{mon}(\mathbf{F})
and their negative pushoffs

In this section, we give an explicit construction of Lagrangian sections
\tilde{L}_{mon}(\mathbf{F})
which are arclike in
S_{0}
.
We construct these sections following
[
Han19
, Proposition 2.13]
. Let
\mathbf{F}:M_{\R}\to\R
be an integral piecewise linear function on
\Sigma^{*}
, that is,
\mathbf{F}
is the support function of a divisor on
Y^{*}
. Let
\eta:M_{\R}\to\R
be a smooth symmetric mollifier function supported on the ball of radius
1
around the origin with respect to an arbitrarily chosen Euclidean metric. Then, for small
\delta>0
, we set
\eta_{\delta}(u):=\delta^{-n}\eta(u/\delta)
and

H_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u):=\int_{M_{\R}}\mathbf{F}(u-y)\eta_{\delta}(y)\,dy.

Definition 35
.

The tropical Lagrangian section associated with
\mathbf{F}
is

\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}):=\text{graph}\left(dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}\right)=\left\{\left(u,dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u)\right):u\in M_{\R}\right\}\subset M_{\R}\times M^{*}_{S^{1}}=T^{*}M^{*}_{S^{1}}\simeq M^{*}_{\C^{*}}.

Sometimes we will drop
\delta
from the notation.

In
[
Han19
]
, these Lagrangian sections are shown to be mirror objects to line bundles in a modified version of
\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}
.
However, it is observed in
[
Han19
, Section 4.4]
that in the Fano setting,
\tilde{L}_{mon}(\mathbf{F})
is also a tropical Lagrangian section in the sense of
[
Abo09
]
, i.e., is an object of
\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}
.

Proposition 36
.

Let
\epsilon>0
. We may choose
\delta
sufficiently small so that:

1.

We have

\left((1-\psi_{\alpha}(t,x))\cdot z^{\alpha}\right)|_{\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})}\in\R_{\geq 0}

(4.4)

for all
\alpha\in A
and all
x
. In particular,
\left(\operatorname{Im}\,W_{t,1}\right)|_{\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})}=0
.

2.

\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is contained in the
\epsilon
-neighbourhood of
{\mathbb{L}}_{\Sigma^{*}}
.

3.

The tangent space to
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is contained in the
\epsilon
-neighbourhood of the set

\displaystyle Q:=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\Sigma^{*}}\sigma\times\sigma^{\perp}\times M_{\R}\times\sigma^{\perp}\subset M_{\R}\times M^{*}_{S^{1}}\times M_{\R}\times M^{*}_{\R}=T(M_{\R}\times M^{*}_{S^{1}}).

(4.5)

Proof 4.10
.

For each nonzero
\alpha\in A
, we have that

dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(\alpha)=\mathbf{F}(\alpha)

(4.6)

in the star of
\alpha
away from a
\delta
-neighbourhood of the boundary. Since
\mathbf{F}(\alpha)\in\Z
, this implies that
\arg(z^{\alpha})|_{\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})}=0
in this region. On the other hand, by
[
Han19
, Corollary 2.41]
, we can choose
\varphi
so that the image under
\Phi
of the region on which
\psi_{\alpha}(t,x)\neq 1
is contained in the interior of the star of
\alpha
.
7
7

7

Note that, from
[
Han19
, Proposition 2.34]
and the discussion following Proposition 2.36 in
loc. cit.
, the condition we impose here on

implies that
\varphi
is adapted to
\Delta^{*}
.
In particular, (
4.6
) holds over all

satisfying
\psi_{\alpha}(t,x)\neq 1
if
\delta
is sufficiently small. Thus, we see that (
4.4
) holds for all nonzero
\alpha
. However, (
4.4
) holds trivially for
\alpha=0
, completing the proof of (
1
).

Suppose
\left(u,dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u)\right)\in\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
. In the complement of a neighbourhood
U_{\delta}
of the origin in
M_{\R}^{*}
whose size is governed by
\delta
, there is a minimal cone
\tau\in\Sigma^{*}
such that
d(u,\tau)<\delta
. Let
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\in A
be the primitive generators of
\tau
. We then have that
dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u)(\alpha_{j})=\mathbf{F}(\alpha_{j})
for
j=1,\ldots,k
. In particular, if
v\in\tau
is a point such that
d(u,v)<\delta
, we have

\left(v,dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u)\right)\in\tau\times\tau^{\perp}\subset{\mathbb{L}}_{\Sigma^{*}}

and the distance between
\left(v,dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u)\right)
and
\left(u,dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u)\right)
is less than
\delta
.
This completes the proof of (
2
).

Finally, observe that in the interior of the set of all
u
with minimal cone
\tau
, we have
dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u)\in\tau^{\perp}
, which implies that the tangent space to the graph of
dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}
is contained in
M_{\R}\times\tau^{\perp}
.
Therefore, the point
(v,dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u),T_{(u,dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u))}\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))
is contained in
Q
, and is distance
\delta
from
(u,dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u),T_{(u,dH_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u))}\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))
as required. The union of the interiors of these sets, over all cones
\tau
, cover the complement of
U
. Thus, we complete the argument by choosing
\delta<\epsilon
sufficiently small that
U_{\delta}\subset B_{\epsilon}(0)
.
This completes the proof of (
3
).

We obtain as a corollary:

Corollary 37
.

For
\delta
sufficiently small and the
I_{0}
constructed in
Lemma

15
, the Lagrangians
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
are
I_{0}
-arclike in
S_{0}
.

Proof 4.11
.

We first check that
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is transverse to the boundary of
S_{0}
. Recall from
Lemma

14
and the discussion following that
S_{0}
is constructed from the data of a sutured Liouville domain
(B,F)
, where
B
is given by modifying
\Log_{t}^{-1}(\mathcal{C}_{0})
.

The Liouville vector field
(u\partial_{u},0)
on
M_{\R}\times M^{*}_{S^{1}}
is transverse to the boundary of
B
by construction.
Therefore, we have
d(u\partial_{u},T\partial B)>\epsilon
for some
\epsilon>0
, where distances are measured with respect to an arbitrary Riemannian metric.
Now let
p\in\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})\cap\partial B
.
As
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is a Lagrangian section, the tangent vector
u\partial_{u}
lifts to a vector
(u\partial_{u},v)\in T_{p}\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
.
By
Proposition

36
, there exists a tangent vector
(\tilde{p},(\tilde{u},\tilde{v}))\in Q
which is close to
(p,(u\partial_{u},v))
.
We may arrange that this tangent vector lies over
\partial B
, by flowing along the Liouville flow.
By choosing
\delta
sufficiently small, we may guarantee that
(p,(u\partial_{u},v))
is arbitrarily close to
(\tilde{p},(\tilde{u},\tilde{v}))
; and as a consequence that
T_{p}\partial B
is arbitrarily close to
T_{\tilde{p}}\partial B
.

Now assume that
(\tilde{p},(\tilde{u},\tilde{v}))\in(\sigma\times\sigma^{\perp})\times(M_{\R}\times\sigma^{\perp})
.
Recall that by construction of
B
,
\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}\cap\partial\Log_{t}^{-1}(\mathcal{C}_{0})\subset\partial B
.
In particular,
\Log_{t}(\tilde{p})\times\sigma^{\perp}\subset\partial B
, and hence
\{0\}\times\sigma^{\perp}\subset T_{\tilde{p}}\partial B
.
We now have:

\displaystyle d\left((p,(u\partial_{u},v)),T_{p}\partial B\right)

\displaystyle>d\left((\tilde{p},(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})),T_{\tilde{p}}\partial B\right)-\epsilon/2\quad\text{for $\delta$ sufficiently small}

\displaystyle=d\left((\tilde{p},(\tilde{u},0)),T_{\tilde{p}}\partial B\right)-\epsilon/2\quad\text{as $\tilde{v}\in\sigma^{\perp}$ so $(0,\tilde{v})\in T_{\tilde{p}}\partial B$}

\displaystyle\geq d\left((\tilde{p},(u\partial_{u},0)),T_{\tilde{p}}\partial B\right)-d\left((\tilde{p},(u\partial_{u},0)),(\tilde{p},(\tilde{u},0))\right)-\epsilon/2

\displaystyle>\epsilon-\epsilon/2-\epsilon/2\quad\text{for $\delta$ sufficiently small}

\displaystyle=0.

Therefore,
(u\partial_{u},v)\in T_{p}\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is transverse to the hypersurface
T\partial B
, so
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is transverse to
\partial B
at
p
, and we may choose
\delta
sufficiently small that this holds for all
p\in\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}\cap\partial B
, by compactness.
We now observe that by construction of
S_{0}
,
\partial S_{0}
agrees with
\partial B
along a neighbourhood of
\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}
.
By choosing
\delta
sufficiently small, we may arrange that
\partial\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is contained in this neighbourhood by
Proposition

36
; thus
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
intersects
\partial S_{0}
transversely as required.

By
Lemma

15
, the zero sets of
I_{0}
and
\operatorname{Im}(W_{t,1})
agree when we are close to both
\partial S_{0}
and
\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}
. By
Proposition

36
:
Items

1
and
2
,
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is contained in the intersection of the zero set of
\operatorname{Im}(W_{t,1})
and a small neighbourhood of
\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}
. Therefore
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})\cap\Nbd(\partial S_{0})\subset I_{0}^{-1}(0)\cap\Nbd(\partial S_{0})
.
Thus,
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is
I_{0}
-arclike in
S_{0}
.

By
Lemma

17
(
3
), we may extend (by the flow of
V
applied to
\partial\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
)
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
to an
I
-arclike Lagrangian

(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))^{\prime}\subset S.

(4.7)

whose intersection with
S_{0}
is the
I_{0}
-arclike
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
(to see
I
-arclikeness of the extension, note that tangency of
(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))^{\prime}
to
V=X_{I}
near
\partial S
implies both transversality to
\partial S
and containment in an
I
-level set by
Lemma

94
).

{rmk}

Here is an equivalent way to define the extension
(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))^{\prime}
of
(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))
.
Observe that
(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))
itself is tangent to
X_{I_{0}}
near and in particular just outside
\partial S_{0}
by
Lemma

15
and
Lemma

94
, and hence determines an
I_{0}
-arclike Lagrangian in the enlarged sector
S_{1}\supset S_{0}
constructed in the proof of
Lemma

17
with deformed Liouville form
\lambda^{\prime}
(in fact
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is tangent to
X_{I_{0}}
over all of
S_{1}\backslash S_{0}^{\circ}
). Now apply
\Phi
, the symplectomorphism from
\lambda^{\prime}
to
\lambda
constructed in the same proof, to obtain
(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))^{\prime}
.

If in the construction of the deformation
\lambda^{\prime}
, we modified
\lambda^{\prime}
by a compactly supported exact 1-form to equal
\lambda
on a compact set containing
(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))\cap(S_{1}\backslash S_{0}^{\circ})
, then we could further arrange that
\Phi=id
near
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
and hence that
(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))^{\prime}=\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
at the expense of slightly modifying
S:=\Phi(S_{1})
.

Since
S
has exact boundary, we can now take the negative pushoff of
(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))^{\prime}
in the sense of
Appendix

A
, using the boundary sector
F\times T^{*}[0,1]\hookrightarrow S
induced by
I
.
The outcome is a cylindrical exact Lagrangian whose boundary avoids
S

\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F}):=((\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))^{\prime})^{-}\subset S;

(4.8)

this Lagrangian, like
(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}))^{\prime}
and
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
, has an anchoring determined by
\mathbf{F}
and can be thought of as an object of
\mathcal{W}(S)^{M^{*}}
.
The modifications involved in taking cylindrical pushoff are only made in
F\times T^{*}[0,1]\subset(S\backslash S_{0})
so in particular

\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})\cap S_{0}=\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}).

(4.9)

We will drop
\delta
from the notation when appropriate.

4.4.2
Cylindrized tropical Lagrangian section:
\tilde{L}_{cyl}(\mathbf{F})

By instead using a “cylindrical smoothing” of
\mathbf{F}
, we can also construct, as in
[
HH22
, Section 3.1]
, a cylindrical Lagrangian section of
M^{*}_{\C^{*}}
whose Legendrian boundary lies in the complement of
\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}}
, that is, an object of
\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})
.
The function
\mathbf{F}
determines an anchoring of this object, i.e., a lift to
\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})^{M^{*}}
.

We define
{G}_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}:M_{\R}\setminus\{0\}\to\mathbb{R}
by

{G}_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u)=\int_{M_{\R}}\mathbf{F}(u-y)\eta_{\delta|u|}(y)\,dy=\int_{M_{\R}}\mathbf{F}(u-|u|y)\eta_{\delta}(y)\,dy

(4.10)

where
|u|
is measured with respect to the Euclidean metric.
It is then shown in
[
HH22
, Lemma 3.1]
that for any
\mathbf{F}
there is a constant
C_{\mathbf{F}}
such that

\left|d{G}_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(\alpha)-\mathbf{F}(\alpha)\right|\leq\delta C_{\mathbf{F}}

on the open cones

U^{\delta}_{\alpha}=\text{cone}\{u\in M_{\R}\;:\;|u|=1,u\in\st(\alpha),\text{ and }d(u,\sigma)>\delta\text{ for all }\sigma\in\Sigma\text{ such that }\alpha\not\in\sigma\}

(4.11)

for all nonzero
\alpha\in A
.
In the language of
[
HH22
, Definition 2.5]
,
U^{\delta}_{\alpha}
is the
\delta
-star of
\alpha
.

Given a support function
\mathbf{F}
for
\Sigma^{*}
, we can set

\mathbf{F}_{a}=\mathbf{F}-a\mathbf{F}_{K}

(4.12)

where
\mathbf{F}_{K}
is the support function of the canonical divisor and
0<a\ll 1
.
We then define a smooth function
H_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}
by smoothing
{G}_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F}_{a},\delta}
arbitrarily on a compact neighbourhood of the origin, which may be taken to be arbitrarily small. We denote such a neighbourhood by
\Nbd(0)
in what follows. The resulting
H_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}
will satisfy

\left|dH_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}(\alpha)-\mathbf{F}(\alpha)+a\right|\leq\delta C_{\mathbf{F}_{a}}

(4.13)

on
U^{\delta}_{\alpha}\setminus\Nbd(0)
. Since
\mathbf{F}(\alpha)
is an integer and
a
is not, we may choose
\delta
small enough that
dH_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}(\alpha)\notin\Z
on
U^{\delta}_{\alpha}\setminus\Nbd(0)
.
On the other hand, if
u\in U^{\delta}_{\alpha}
and
(u,\theta)\in\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}
, then
\theta(\alpha)\in\Z
.
It follows that the Lagrangian

\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F}):=\text{graph}\left(dH_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}\right)

avoids
{\mathbb{L}}_{\Sigma^{*}}
outside of the compact set
\pi^{-1}(\Nbd(0))
, for
\delta
sufficiently small.
Thus,
\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
determines an object of
\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})
. Observe that all choices of
a,\delta
with
0<a\ll 1
and
\delta
sufficiently small yield admissibly Hamiltonian isotopic Lagrangians in
M^{*}_{\C^{*}}
; we will therefore drop
a
and
\delta
from the notation when appropriate.

4.4.3
Comparing constructions of Lagrangian sections

In the previous two subsections we have constructed two a priori different cylindrical Lagrangians in
\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})
associated to
\mathbf{F}
: the cylindrized tropical section
\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})

and the negative pushoff of the tropical arclike Lagrangian
\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})

(strictly speaking, each of these depend on small parameters, but only up to
cylindrical isotopy hence isomorphism in the Fukaya category).

Proposition

40
below asserts that they are isomorphic. The obvious
isotopy between these two objects is non-cylindrical (passing through the
non-pushed off
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
), so doesn’t immediately induce an isomorphism.
Instead, we show two objects can be made (after a compactly supported isotopy)
to agree with the arclike Lagrangian
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
on a subsector
S_{0}
, and
also that both objects cofinally wrap away from the subsector. From here,
locality theory for arclike Lagrangians in Liouville sectors,
developed in
Appendix

A
, shows that when tested against objects coming
from the Fukaya category of the (quasi-equivalent) subsector
S_{0}
, all disks
appearing for either Lagrangian also remain in the subsector, and therefore
coincide.

Conceptually, we may think of
\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
and

\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})
as “generalized negative pushoffs” of the
same arclike
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
, in the sense that they agree with
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})

within an equivalent subsector which they cofinally wrap away from.
The technology of
Appendix

A
can be used to show that any two
generalized negative pushoffs of an arclike Lagrangian are isomorphic objects;
compare
Section

A.4
.

Lemma 38

(
[
HH22
]
, Lemma 3.3)
.

\partial_{\infty}\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
admits a cofinal wrapping in
(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})
converging to
\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}}
which does not cross
\partial_{\infty}S_{0}
.

Proof 4.12
.

The cofinal wrapping is exhibited in loc. cit.. As also shown there, the boundary at infinity of
\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
and its wrappings can be made to take place in an arbitrarily small neighborhood of
\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}}
, which is, in particular, disjoint from
\partial_{\infty}S_{0}
.

Proposition 39
.

There is a Lagrangian
\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
in
M^{*}_{\C^{*}}
which differs from
\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
by a compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopy and satisfies

\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})\cap S_{0}=\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})

for sufficiently small
\delta
and
a
.

Proof 4.13
.

Pick
\epsilon>0
and
R>0
so that
\pi(S_{0}\cap B_{\epsilon}(\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}))\subset B_{R}(0)\subset M_{\R}
.
Let
\rho_{1}:M_{\R}\to\R_{\geq 0}
be smooth, equal to
|u|
outside
B_{R+b}(0)
for some
b>0
, and equal to
1
on
B_{R}(0)
, and let
\rho_{2}:M_{\R}\to[0,1]
be smooth, equal to
1
outside
B_{R+b}(0)
, and equal to
0
on
B_{R}(0)
.

We now perform a similar construction to (
4.10
) by defining

{G}_{mc}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}(u)=\int_{M_{\R}}\mathbf{F}(u-y)\eta_{\delta\rho_{1}(u)}(y)\,dy

(4.14)

for
u\in M_{\R}
.
We note that
{G}_{mc}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}
agrees with
{G}_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}
on the complement of
B_{R+b}(0)
, and with
H_{mon}^{\mathbf{F},\delta}
on
B_{R}(0)
. We now set

H_{mc}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}(u)=G^{\mathbf{F},\delta}_{mc}(u)-\rho_{2}(u)aG^{\mathbf{F}_{K},\delta}_{mc}(u)

for all
u\in M_{\R}
and define
\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
to be the graph of
dH_{mc}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}
. As before, we note that
H^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}_{mc}
agrees with
H^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}_{cyl}
in the complement of
B_{R+b}(0)
and with
H^{\mathbf{F},\delta}_{mon}
on
B_{R}(0)
.

To show that
\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
agrees with
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
on
S_{0}
, it suffices to show that it lies in a small neighbourhood of
\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}}
as we have chosen
\epsilon
and
R
so that
S_{0}\cap B_{\epsilon}(\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}})\subset\pi^{-1}(B_{R}(0))
.
By applying the same argument as in
Proposition

36
, we can deduce that there exists a choice of
\delta
and
a
so that
\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is contained in
B_{\epsilon}(\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}})
by obtaining a uniform bound on
|dH_{mc}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}(\alpha)-\mathbf{F}(\alpha)|
on
U^{\delta}_{\alpha}\setminus\Nbd(0)
for all
\alpha\in A
that goes to zero as
\delta
and
a
go to zero.

We will now produce such a bound. Let
\mathbf{F}_{\sigma}
be the linear function given by restricting
\mathbf{F}
to any
\sigma\in\Sigma^{*}(\dim Y^{*})
. Then, for all
u\in U^{\delta}_{\alpha}\setminus\Nbd(0)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|(dG_{mc}^{\mathbf{F},\delta})_{u}(\alpha)-\mathbf{F}(\alpha)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|d\rho_{1}(\alpha)\right|\left|\int_{M_{\R}}d\mathbf{F}_{u-\rho_{1}(u)y}(y)\eta_{\delta}(y)\,dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|d\rho_{1}(\alpha)\right|\int_{M_{\R}}\left|d\mathbf{F}_{u-\rho_{1}(u)y}\right|\left|y\right|\eta_{\delta}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\delta|d\rho_{1}(\alpha)|\max_{\sigma\in\Sigma^{*}(\dim Y^{*})}\left|\mathbf{F}_{\sigma}\right|

which uniformly tends to
0
as
\delta
goes to zero as
|d\rho_{1}|
is bounded. The same calculation shows that for all
u\in U^{\delta}_{\alpha}
with
|u|\geq R+b
(so that
\rho_{2}\equiv 1
) we have

\left|d\left(\rho_{2}aG^{\mathbf{F}_{K},\delta}_{mc}\right)(\alpha)-a\right|\leq\delta C

for some constant
C
. Finally, inside of
B_{R+b}(0)
, the norm of
d\left(\rho_{2}G^{\mathbf{F}_{K},\delta}_{mc}\right)
is bounded so

\left|d\left(\rho_{2}aG^{\mathbf{F}_{K},\delta}_{mc}\right)\right|\leq aC^{\prime}

for some constant
C^{\prime}
. Putting all these estimates together, we obtain the desired bound on
|dH_{mc}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}(\alpha)-\mathbf{F}(\alpha)|
.

Therefore, we have shown that
\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})\subset B_{\epsilon}(\mathbb{L}_{\Sigma^{*}})
for any small
\epsilon>0
.
In particular, it follows that

\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})\cap S_{0}=\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})\cap S_{0}\cap\pi^{-1}(B_{R}(0))=\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})

as required. We also have, by construction, that the flow of
H_{mc}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}-H_{cyl}^{\mathbf{F},a,\delta}
is a Hamiltonian isotopy, supported in the compact set
\pi^{-1}(B_{R+b}(0))
, from
\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
to
\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
.

Proposition 40
.

There is an isomorphism of objects of
\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})^{M^{*}}

between
\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
and
\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})
.

Proof 4.14
.

We will use the following notation for the tautological sector embeddings:
i:S_{0}\to S
,
i^{\prime}:S\to(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})
and
i^{\prime}\circ i:S_{0}\to(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})
. The induced pushforward functors on wrapped Fukaya categories are all equivalences indicated by the left and right vertical arrows in
Figure

1
.

\tilde{L}_{cyl}
\tilde{L}

\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}
(i^{\prime}\circ i)^{*}\hom_{\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})}(\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta},-)
\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}\cap S_{0}=\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}=\tilde{L}\cap S_{0}
CF^{*}(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta},-)

\cap S_{0}
\cap S_{0}

39

\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})^{M^{*}}

:
Schematic diagram of the proof of

.

We prove the isomorphism by exhibiting isomorphisms indicated in
Figure

1
by horizontal arrows.
First,
\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
is isomorphic to
\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
(which satisfies all of the hypotheses of
Proposition

39
) because they differ by a compactly-supported Hamiltonian isotopy. Now, since
\partial_{\infty}\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})=\partial_{\infty}\tilde{L}_{cyl}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
possesses a cofinal wrapping which does not cross
\partial S_{0}
by

38
, and since
\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})\cap S_{0}
is the
I_{0}
-arclike Lagrangian
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
,

100
applied to
i^{\prime}\circ i:S_{0}\to(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})
implies that there is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{W}(S_{0})
modules

(i^{\prime}\circ i)^{*}\hom_{\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})}(\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F}),-)\cong CF^{*}(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}),-),

(4.15)

where
CF^{*}(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}),-)
is the “arclike”
\mathcal{W}(S_{0})
-module constructed by applying
Lemma

98
to
\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
.

Similarly, the negative pushoff
\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})
also satisfies
\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})\cap S_{0}=\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F})
(see (
4.9
)), and
\partial_{\infty}\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})

\partial_{\infty}(F\times T^{*}[0,1])\subset\partial_{\infty}S
which in particular avoids
\partial_{\infty}S_{0}
(see
Lemma

). Hence,
Lemma

100
, this time applied to
i:S_{0}\to S
again implies that

i^{*}\hom_{\mathcal{W}(S)}(\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F}),-)\cong CF^{*}(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}),-).

(4.16)

Since we can view
\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})
as living in
\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})
via the pushforward
i^{\prime}
, and
i^{\prime}
is a quasi-equivalence, we also know
\hom_{\mathcal{W}(S)}(\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F}),-)\cong(i^{\prime})^{*}\hom_{\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})}(\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F}),-)
, from which we collectively deduce that

(i^{\prime}\circ i)^{*}\hom_{\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})}(\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F}),-)\cong CF^{*}(\tilde{L}_{mon}^{\delta}(\mathbf{F}),-);

(4.17)

in other words, (
4.15
) and (
4.17
) imply that

(i^{\prime}\circ i)^{*}\hom_{\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})}(\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F}),-)\cong(i^{\prime}\circ i)^{*}\hom_{\mathcal{W}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}},\mathfrak{f}_{\Sigma^{*}})}(\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F}),-).

Now,
i^{\prime}\circ i
is a quasi-equivalence, so the same isomorphism holds before applying
(i^{\prime}\circ i)^{*}
. Therefore Yoneda implies
\tilde{L}(\mathbf{F})\cong\tilde{L}_{mc}^{a,\delta}(\mathbf{F})
.

5
Powers of an ample line bundle

From here on, we drop decorations and use
\cap:\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}\to\partial\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}
to denote the cap functor which makes the diagram from
Proposition

33
commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(\partial Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pic_{dg}^{M^{*}}(Y^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{mon}^{M^{*}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap}

(5.1)

Recall that all categories have objects indexed by the integral piecewise-linear functions
\mathbf{F}
.

Let
\mathbf{F}_{\Delta}
denote the function corresponding to the toric boundary divisor; that is,
\mathbf{F}_{\Delta}
is equal to
-1
on the generator of each ray of
\Sigma^{*}
.
We now restrict the diagram (
5.1
) to the respective subcategories with objects indexed by
k\mathbf{F}_{\Delta}
.
We denote the corresponding subcategories by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{0}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap}

(5.2)

The objects of
\mathcal{B}_{0}
are
\mathcal{O}(k)=i^{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y^{*}}(k\mathbf{F}_{\Delta})
and those of
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
are
\mathcal{E}_{k}=\mathcal{O}_{Y^{*}}((k+1)\mathbf{F}_{\Delta})[1]
so that
i^{!}\mathcal{E}_{k}=\mathcal{O}(k)
(see, for example,
[
Huy06
, Section 3.4]
).
The objects of
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
are the mirror tropical Lagrangian sections
\tilde{L}_{k}
to
\mathcal{E}_{k}
, and those of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
are
L_{k}=\cap\tilde{L}_{k}
(their boundaries).
In an
A_{\infty}
category
\mathcal{C}
, we use the convention that the cochain-level morphism spaces are denoted
hom^{*}_{\mathcal{C}}(-,-)
, and their cohomologies by
Hom^{*}_{\mathcal{C}}(-,-)
.

The main result of this Section is Lemma
49
, which shows that the quasi-isomorphism
F_{0}
above can be made to respect the natural
\mathbb{Z}\oplus M^{*}
-gradings on both sides. Along the way, we also prove (Corollary
44
) that the closed–open map

\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}:SH^{*}(F;\Bbbk)\to\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{0})

from the symplectic cohomology of
F
to the Hochschild cohomology of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
is a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded Lie algebra isomorphism.

5.1
Hochschild invariants and Calabi–Yau structure

The symplectic cohomology of
F
admits a grading by
\Z\oplus M^{*}
, where the
\Z
component comes from the Conley–Zehnder index and the
M^{*}=H_{1}(M^{*}_{\C^{*}})
component comes from the homology class of the orbit included into
M^{*}_{\C^{*}}
. Note that because
\mathcal{W}(F)^{M^{*}}
is
\Z\oplus M^{*}
graded, we can define a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded version of
\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{W}(F)^{M^{*}})
(cf. Section
C.1
).

Proposition 41
.

The closed–open map

\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}:SH^{*}(F;\Bbbk)\to\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{W}(F)^{M^{*}})

is a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded isomorphism.

Proof 5.1
.

Given simply connected Lagrangians
L_{0},\ldots,L_{k}
and intersections
x_{i}\in L_{i-1}\cap L_{i}
with
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
and
x_{0}\in L_{0}\cap L_{k}
, we compute the
M^{*}
-grading of the Hochschild cochain
(x_{1}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{k}^{\vee})\otimes x_{0}
. First, pick paths
\gamma_{i}:I\to L_{i}
from
x_{i}
to
x_{i+1}
. Then the grading of the Hochschild cochain is the homology class of the loop
\gamma_{0}\cdots\gamma_{k}
.

The structure coefficient of the closed-open map evaluated at
y
an orbit and
(x_{1}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{k}^{\vee})\otimes x_{0}
a Hochschild cochain is given by a count of decorated
J
-holomorphic disks. The decorations impose the following constraints: an interior marked point labels a puncture limiting towards the orbit
y
; cyclically labelled boundary marked points are required to map to the
x_{i}
. Additionally, the
(x_{i},x_{i+1})
component of the boundary maps to
L_{i}
. It is immediate that
[y]=[\partial D^{2}]=[\gamma_{0}\cdots\gamma_{k}]
.

To check that
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}:SH^{*}(F;\Bbbk)\to\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{W}(F))
is an isomorphism, it suffices by
[
Gan13
, Theorem 1.1]
to show that
\mathcal{O}\mathcal{C}:HH_{*-n}(\mathcal{W}(F))\to SH^{*}(F)
hits the identity; this holds because the map is an isomorphism
[
Cha+24
,
GPS24a
,
Gan13
,
Gao17
]
.
As the wrapped Fukaya category is generated by cocores of critical handles
[
Cha+24
,
GPS24a
]
which are contractible and therefore admit anchorings, one can carry out the same argument for the
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded versions.

Recall that (after shifting
\mathbb{Z}
-gradings up by one) symplectic cohomology has the structure of a graded Lie algebra (see e.g.
[
LP16
, §2.1]
). This Lie algebra structure is compatible with
M^{*}
-gradings. Hochschild cohomology also has a graded Lie algebra structure (again after shifting
\mathbb{Z}
-gradings up by one; see
Eq.

C.2
) which is also compatible with
M^{*}
gradings.

Lemma 42
.

In the situation of
Proposition

41
,
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}
intertwines Lie algebra structures.

Proof 5.2
.

This is a now-standard argument involving using closed-open moduli spaces to construct a homotopy between
[\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}(-),\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}(-)]
and
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}([-,-])
, analogous to the argument that
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}
is a ring homomorphism
[
Gan13
, Proposition 5.3]
. The relevant geometric moduli spaces appear as a special case (
m=2
,
d
arbitrary) of the moduli spaces used in
[
PS23a
, Proposition 6.2.1]
(the relevant statements of results in loc. cit. are about the existence of bulk-deformed closed-open maps, but the geometric moduli spaces in particular show
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebra homomorphism, which is more than we need).

{rmk}

The definition of wrapped Fukaya categories in
[
GPS20
, §3]
uses a ‘(categorical) localization’ model for wrapped Floer theory. On the other hand, the construction of closed-open (and open-closed) maps in
[
Gan13
]
uses quadratic Hamiltonians for the construction of wrapped Floer groups (and symplectic cohomology). These two models for the wrapped Fukaya category are shown to be canonically quasi-equivalent in
[
Syl19
, Proposition 2.6]
.

Proposition 43
.

The restriction map

\displaystyle\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{W}(F)^{M^{*}})\to\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{0})

(5.3)

is a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded isomorphism.

Proof 5.3
.

Let
\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\sharp})
denote the Hochschild cohomology of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
viewed only as a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded category (in other words, forgetting
M^{*}
-gradings). We first argue that the restriction map

\displaystyle\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{W}(F))\to\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\sharp})

(5.4)

is an isomorphism. To see this, by
Proposition

33
(
4
), it suffices to check that the restriction map from the Hochschild cohomology of
D^{b}Coh(\partial Y^{*})
to that of the full subcategory with objects
\mathcal{O}(i)
is an isomorphism. This follows from
[
Pre11
, Corollary B.5.1(i)]
. The same corollary also implies that
\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\sharp})
is finite-dimensional in each
\mathbb{Z}
-grading (because it’s isomorphic to endomorphisms of the diagonal
\Delta\subset\partial Y^{*}\times\partial Y^{*}
). Because each of the hom-spaces in
\mathcal{A}_{0}
is finite dimensional over
\Bbbk
, we have by (
C.6
) that

\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\sharp})\cong\prod_{\mathsf{q}\in M^{*}}\mathrm{HH}^{*\oplus\mathsf{q}}(\mathcal{A}_{0})\cong\bigoplus_{\mathsf{q}\in M^{*}}\mathrm{HH}^{*\oplus\mathsf{q}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}),

where the second isomorphism uses the aforementioned finite-dimensionality of
\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\sharp}).
From the proof of Proposition
41
, we also have that
\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{W}(F))
is a direct sum of its
M^{*}
-graded pieces. As (
5.4
) is an isomorphism, it follows that (
5.3
) is as well.

Combining
Proposition

41
and
Proposition

43
with the fact that the restriction map on Hochschild cohomology is a Lie algebra homomorphism, we obtain:

Corollary 44
.

The closed–open map

\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}:SH^{*}(F;\Bbbk)\to\mathrm{HH}^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{0})

is a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded isomorphism of Lie algebras.∎

We recall that a linear map
\phi:HH_{n}(\mathcal{C})\to\Bbbk
is called a
weak proper
n
-Calabi–Yau structure
on
\mathcal{C}
if the pairing

\displaystyle(-,-)_{\phi}:Hom^{i}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)\otimes Hom^{n-i}_{\mathcal{C}}(Y,X)

\displaystyle\to\Bbbk

\displaystyle(a,b)_{\phi}

\displaystyle:=\phi([a\bullet b])

is a perfect pairing, for all pairs of objects
X
and
Y
and
i\in\Z
.

Compact Fukaya categories of
2n
-dimensional Liouville domains always admit weak proper
n
-Calabi–Yau structures
[
Sei08
, (12j)]
, and in particular,
\mathcal{A}_{0}
does.

Lemma 45
.

HH_{n}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
has rank 1 and is concentrated in degree
0\in M^{*}
. Furthermore, the map
Hom^{n}_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}(L_{i},L_{i})\to HH_{n}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
is an isomorphism for any
i
.

Proof 5.4
.

The weak proper
n
-Calabi–Yau structure on
\mathcal{A}_{0}
induces an isomorphism

HH_{i}(\mathcal{A}_{0})\cong HH^{n-i}(\mathcal{A}_{0})^{\vee}

(see
[
She16
, Lemma A.2]
).
Applying Corollary
44
, we find that
HH_{n}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
is dual to
SH^{0}(F)
, which has rank
1
spanned by the unit in this case, by a straightforward application of
[
GP20
, Theorem 1.1]
(see §
6.1
for further discussion of these results).

We now note that as the composition

Hom^{0}(L_{i},L_{i})\otimes Hom^{n}(L_{i},L_{i})\to Hom^{n}(L_{i},L_{i})\to HH_{n}(\mathcal{A}_{0})\xrightarrow{\phi}\Bbbk

is a perfect pairing, it must be non-zero.
As both
Hom^{n}(L_{i},L_{i})
and
HH_{n}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
have rank
1
, the map
Hom^{n}(L_{i},L_{i})\to HH_{n}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
must be an isomorphism.

This map clearly respects the
M^{*}
-grading, and
Hom^{n}(L_{i},L_{i})
is concentrated in degree
0\in M^{*}
for any
i
, hence
HH_{n}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
is too.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded
A_{\infty}
category, then a graded weak proper
n
-Calabi–Yau structure is a weak proper
n
-Calabi–Yau structure
\phi
which vanishes on
HH_{n\oplus\mathsf{p}}
for
\mathsf{p}\neq 0
.
In this case, the pairing on morphism spaces respects the
M^{*}
-grading.

Lemma 46
.

Both
\mathcal{A}_{0}
and
\mathcal{B}_{0}
admit
M^{*}
-graded weak proper
n
-Calabi–Yau structures, which are respected by the isomorphism
HH_{n}(\mathcal{A}_{0})=HH_{n}(\mathcal{B}_{0})
induced by the quasi-isomorphism
\mathcal{A}_{0}\simeq\mathcal{B}_{0}
.

Proof 5.5
.

The weak proper
n
-Calabi–Yau structure on
\mathcal{A}_{0}
induces one on
\mathcal{B}_{0}
via the isomorphism
HH_{n}(\mathcal{A}_{0})=HH_{n}(\mathcal{B}_{0})
.
By Lemma
45
,
HH_{n}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
is concentrated in degree
0\in M^{*}
, hence the Calabi–Yau structure on
\mathcal{A}_{0}
is
M^{*}
-graded (indeed, the weak proper Calabi–Yau structure on the
M^{*}
-graded compact Fukaya category is always
M^{*}
-graded for general reasons).
On
\mathcal{B}_{0}
, we observe that the map
Hom^{n}_{\mathcal{B}_{0}}(\mathcal{O}(i),\mathcal{O}(i))\to HH_{n}(\mathcal{B}_{0})
is
M^{*}
-graded, and an isomorphism by Lemma
45
.
As
Hom^{n}_{\mathcal{B}_{0}}(\mathcal{O}(i),\mathcal{O}(i))=H^{n}(\mathcal{O})
is graded in degree
0\in M^{*}
for any
i
, so is
HH_{n}(\mathcal{B}_{0})
, hence the Calabi–Yau structure on
\mathcal{B}_{0}
is also
M^{*}
-graded.

5.2
Upgrading to an
M^{*}
-graded quasi-isomorphism

Lemma 47
.

The maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Hom^{k}_{\mathcal{B}_{0}}(\mathcal{O}(i),\mathcal{O}(j))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Hom^{k}_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}(L_{i},L_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Hom^{k}_{\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{0}}(\mathcal{E}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Hom^{k}_{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{0}}(\tilde{L}_{i},\tilde{L}_{j})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap}

(5.5)

arising in the diagram (
5.2
) have the following properties:

1.

Hom^{k}(\mathcal{O}(i),\mathcal{O}(j))=Hom^{k}(L_{i},L_{j})=0
unless
k=0
or
n
.

2.

the vertical maps are surjective for
k=0
;

3.

the top arrows respect the Serre duality pairings induced by the Calabi–Yau structures chosen in Lemma
46
in the sense that

(F^{i}_{0}a,F^{n-i}_{0}b)=(a,b)

for any
a\in Hom^{i}_{\mathcal{B}_{0}}(\mathcal{O}(j),\mathcal{O}(k))
and
b\in Hom_{\mathcal{B}_{0}}^{n-i}(\mathcal{O}(k),\mathcal{O}(j))
.

Proof 5.6
.

Items (
1
) and (
2
) are easily verified on
\mathcal{B}_{0}
, and the result follows for
\mathcal{A}_{0}
by commutativity of the diagram. Item (
3
) follows from the fact that the Calabi–Yau structures are chosen so as to be respected by the mirror equivalence.

Lemma 48
.

The cohomological isomorphism
H^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{0})\simeq H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{0})
respects
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-gradings.

Proof 5.7
.

As
\cap
,
i^{!}
, and the bottom arrow in the diagram (
5.5
) respect the
M^{*}
-grading, it follows from Lemma
47
(
2
) that
F_{0}^{0}
respects the
M^{*}
-grading.
It follows from the
i=0
case of Lemma
47
(
3
), together with the fact that the pairings respect the
M^{*}
-grading because the Calabi–Yau structures were chosen in Lemma
46
to be
M^{*}
-graded, that the
F^{n}_{0}
respect the
M^{*}
-grading too.

Lemma 49
.

There exists a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded quasi-isomorphism
\mathcal{A}_{0}\simeq\mathcal{B}_{0}
inducing the isomorphism from Lemma
48
at the level of cohomology.

Proof 5.8
.

This is Lemma
132
.

5.3
Split-generating the compact Fukaya category

In this short subsection, we prove a split-generation result — and therefore an HMS result — for compact exact Lagrangians in
F
which is not needed elsewhere in the text, but which may be of independent interest. We let
\EuF(F)
denote the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded compact (exact) Fukaya category of
F
. Let
\perf_{\mathsf{dg}}(\partial Y^{*})\subset D^{b}_{\mathsf{dg}}Coh(\partial Y^{*})
the full subcategory of perfect complexes (see
Section

8.4
for an explicit dg model of
\perf_{\mathsf{dg}}(-)
, and also compare
Section

8.4
).

Proposition 50
.

The subcategories
\mathcal{B}_{0}\subset\perf(\partial Y^{*})
and
\mathcal{A}_{0}\subset\EuF(F)
split-generate, and in particular, we have a quasi-equivalence

\perf\EuF(F)\simeq\perf_{\mathsf{dg}}(\partial Y^{*}).

Proof 5.9
.

Because the objects of
\mathcal{B}_{0}
are powers of an ample line bundle, they split-generate
\perf_{\mathsf{dg}}(\partial Y^{*})
by
[
Orl09
, Theorem 4]
.

View
L_{i}
as objects of
\mathcal{W}(F)
. By
Proposition

33
(
4
), the equivalence
GS
takes
L_{i}
to the line bundles
\mathcal{O}(i)
. Now consider any other object
E
of
\mathcal{F}(F)\subset\mathcal{W}(F).
Let
GS(E)
denote the image of
E
under the equivalence
GS
. For any object
B\in\operatorname{Ob}(D^{b}_{\mathsf{dg}}Coh(\partial Y^{*}))
, we have that:

\displaystyle\operatorname{dim}_{\Bbbk}(H^{*}(\operatorname{Hom}^{*}_{D^{b}_{\mathsf{dg}}Coh(\partial Y^{*})}(GS(E),B)))<\infty.

(5.6)

It is well-known (
[
Efi17
, Proof of Prop B.1]
or
[
BZNP17
, Remark 1.2.6]
) that this implies that
GS(E)
is perfect. Hence
GS(E)
is split-generated by
\mathcal{O}(i)
. It follows that
E
is split-generated by
L_{i}
.

6
Deforming the
A
-side

In this section, we make the computations needed to show that the subcategory
\mathcal{A}_{R}
of the relative Fukaya category
\EuF(X_{t,\kappa},D,\mathsf{N})
having the same objects as
\mathcal{A}_{0}
, is a versal deformation of the latter.
That is, we verify the hypotheses of the versality criterion from
[
Gan+24
, Proposition A.6]
(the key results are
Lemma

62
,

Proposition

63
, and
Lemma

64
).
In order to make these computations, we identify Hochschild cohomology with symplectic cohomology (via
Corollary

44
), which in turn is identified with log symplectic cohomology by
[
GP21
,
GP20
]
.
We also prove a new result,
Proposition

61
, which identifies the classes in log symplectic cohomology which correspond to the first-order deformation classes under this identification.
The required computations then take place in the log symplectic cohomology, where they are purely topological.

6.1
Symplectic cohomology

We wish to understand
SH^{*}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0})
using the log PSS map from
[
GP21
,
GP20
]
. We let
H^{*}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
denote the log cohomology of the pair
(X_{t,\kappa},D)
, introduced in
[
GP21
, §3.2]
.
H^{*}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
is a certain
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded
\Bbbk
-module defined topologically from the pair
(X_{t,\kappa},D).
We denote the
M^{*}
-graded pieces of
H^{*}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
by
H^{*\oplus\mathsf{q}}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
for
\mathsf{q}\in M^{*}
.

Recall that the generators of
H^{*}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
have the form
\alpha\mathbf{t}^{\mathsf{v}}
where
\mathsf{v}\in\Z_{\geq 0}^{P}
8
8

8

Note that here we use the connectedness condition.
and
\alpha\in H^{*}(S_{\mathsf{v}})
for some manifold
S_{\mathsf{v}}
, which is empty if and only if the corresponding stratum

\displaystyle\bigcap_{\mathsf{p}:v_{\mathsf{p}}\neq 0}D_{\mathsf{p}}

(6.1)

is empty.
We will call
\mathsf{v}
such that
S_{\mathsf{v}}\neq\emptyset

contributing
.

By
[
GP21
, Equation (3.20)]
, the
\Z
-degree of such a chain is

\displaystyle\deg_{\Z}\left(\alpha\mathbf{t}^{\mathsf{v}}\right)=\deg(\alpha)+2|\mathsf{v}|

(6.2)

(where
|\mathsf{v}|:=\sum_{\mathsf{p}\in P}v_{\mathsf{p}}
), while the
M^{*}
-degree is

\displaystyle\deg_{M^{*}}(\mathsf{v})=\sum_{\mathsf{p}\in P}v_{\mathsf{p}}\cdot\mathsf{p}.

(6.3)

Lemma 51
.

If
\mathsf{v}_{1}
and
\mathsf{v}_{2}
are both contributing, and
\deg_{M^{*}}(\mathsf{v}_{1})=\deg_{M^{*}}(\mathsf{v}_{2})
, then
\mathsf{v}_{1}=\mathsf{v}_{2}
.
In particular, if
\mathsf{v}
is contributing, then we have

\displaystyle H^{*\oplus\deg_{M^{*}}(\mathsf{v})}(X_{t,\kappa},D)\cong H^{*-2|\mathsf{v}|}(S_{\mathsf{v}}).

(6.4)

Proof 6.1
.

Note that if
\mathsf{v}
is contributing, then
\deg_{M^{*}}(\mathsf{v})
lies in the relative interior of the cone of
\Sigma_{\kappa}
corresponding to the toric orbit of
Y_{\kappa}
in which (
6.1
) lies.
Furthermore, as
\Sigma_{\kappa}
is simplicial, the generators
\mathsf{p}
for which
v_{\mathsf{p}}\neq 0
form a basis for this cone.
In particular, the point
\deg_{M^{*}}(\mathsf{v})
determines the non-zero coordinates
v_{\mathsf{p}}
; the other coordinates are all
0
.
Thus the
M^{*}
-degree of a contributing
\mathsf{v}
determines
\mathsf{v}
uniquely, as required.

Lemma 52
.

We have:

(i)

In
M^{*}
-grading
0
:

\displaystyle H^{*\oplus 0}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)\cong H^{*}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0}).

(6.5)

(ii)

In
M^{*}
-grading
\mathsf{p}
, for
\mathsf{p}\in P
:

\displaystyle H_{log}^{*\oplus\mathsf{p}}(X_{t,\kappa},D)\cong H^{*-2}(SD_{\mathsf{p}}),

(6.6)

where
SD_{\mathsf{p}}
denotes the normal
S^{1}
bundle to the interior of
D_{\mathsf{p}}
. In particular, for
\mathsf{p}\in P
,
H^{2\oplus\mathsf{p}}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
has rank one, generated by the class
\gamma_{\mathsf{p}}
corresponding to the identity in
H^{0}(SD_{\mathsf{p}})
.

(iii)

In
M^{*}
-grading
\mathsf{q}\notin P\cup\{0\}
,
H_{log}^{i\oplus\mathsf{q}}(X_{t,\kappa},D)=0
for
i\leq 3
.

Proof 6.2
.

Given Lemma
51
,
Item

(i)
follows from the case
\mathsf{v}=0
of (
6.4
), while
Item

(ii)
follows from the case where
v_{\mathsf{q}}=\delta_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q}}
.

Item

(iii)
follows from (
6.2
) and the fact that all other
\mathsf{v}
have
|\mathsf{v}|\geq 2
.

The log cohomology
H^{*}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
carries a
\Z\oplus M^{*}
-graded ring structure (see
[
GP20
, Definition 3.4]
), which we recall in the special case that we need for our applications. Let
\mathring{X}_{\mathsf{p}}\subset X_{t,\kappa}
be the complement of all of the divisor components different from
D_{\mathsf{p}}.
The real oriented blowup
\hat{X}_{\mathsf{p}}
of
\mathring{X}_{\mathsf{p}}
along the open stratum of
D_{\mathsf{p}}
is then a manifold with boundary
SD_{\mathsf{p}}
. The blowup
\hat{X}_{\mathsf{p}}
is homotopy equivalent to
\mathcal{H}_{t,0}
and there is thus a canonically defined restriction map:

\displaystyle r_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}:H^{*}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0})\to H^{*}(SD_{\mathsf{p}}).

(6.7)

Given
\alpha_{1}\in H^{q_{1}\oplus 0}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)=H^{q_{1}}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0})
and
\alpha_{2}\in H^{q_{2}\oplus\mathsf{p}}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)=H^{q_{2}-2}(SD_{\mathsf{p}})
, their product is given by

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}\ast_{log}\alpha_{2}:=r_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}(\alpha_{1})\cup\alpha_{2}\in H_{log}^{q_{1}+q_{2}\oplus\mathsf{p}}(X_{t,\kappa},D)=H^{q_{1}+q_{2}-2}(SD_{\mathsf{p}}).

(6.8)

To define the log PSS map, we rule out sphere bubbling using the following lemma:

Lemma 53
.

Fix
\mathsf{p}\in P
. We can represent the Kähler class
[\omega]=[\omega_{\kappa}]
as a sum

\displaystyle[\omega]=\sum_{\mathsf{q}\in P}\kappa^{\prime}_{\mathsf{q}}\cdot PD(D_{\mathsf{q}}),

(6.9)

where
\kappa^{\prime}_{\mathsf{q}}>0
for
\mathsf{q}\neq\mathsf{p}
, and
\kappa^{\prime}_{\mathsf{p}}=0
.

Proof 6.3
.

The Kähler form is restricted from the ambient toric variety
Y_{\kappa}
. On
Y_{\kappa}
, a class is Kähler if and only if the piecewise-linear function
\phi_{\kappa}
with value
\kappa_{\mathsf{q}}
on the generator
v_{\mathsf{q}}
of the ray of the fan corresponding to
D_{\mathsf{q}}
, is strictly convex. Moreover, we have
[\omega_{\kappa}]=[\omega_{\kappa^{\prime}}]
iff
\phi_{\kappa}-\phi_{\kappa^{\prime}}
is linear. In particular, given a Kähler class
[\omega]
, we can choose
\kappa^{\prime}
so that
\kappa^{\prime}_{\mathsf{p}}=0
and
\kappa^{\prime}_{\mathsf{q}}>0
for all
\mathsf{q}\neq\mathsf{p}
, by adding an appropriate linear function to
\phi_{\kappa}
(cf.
[
She20a
, Lemma 3.31]
).

Corollary 54
.

Let
\tilde{\omega}
be a symplectic form with
[\tilde{\omega}]=[\omega]
,
J
an almost-complex structure compatible with
\tilde{\omega}
, and
\tilde{D}_{\mathsf{p}}
submanifolds homologous to
D_{\mathsf{p}}
for
\mathsf{p}\in P
.
Then any stable
J
-holomorphic curve
u
, with at least one non-constant component, satisfies
u\cdot\tilde{D}_{\mathsf{p}}>0
for at least two of the
\mathsf{p}\in P
.

Proof 6.4
.

Suppose, to the contrary, that there exists
\mathsf{p}\in P
such that
u\cdot\tilde{D}_{\mathsf{q}}\leq 0
for
\mathsf{q}\neq\mathsf{p}
.
Then by
Lemma

53
, we have

\displaystyle 0\geq\sum_{\mathsf{q}\in P}\kappa^{\prime}_{\mathsf{q}}\tilde{D}_{\mathsf{q}}\cdot u=\tilde{\omega}(u)>0,

(6.10)

a contradiction.

We recall that the construction of the log PSS map involves deforming
\omega
to a symplectic form
\tilde{\omega}
, within the same cohomology class, so that the divisor
D
admits a regularization in the sense of
[
MTZ18
, Definition 2.10]
(which builds on
[
McL12
, §5]
). Corollary
54
implies that for each
\mathsf{p}\in P
, any
J
-holomorphic sphere bubble (for any almost-complex structure
J
compatible with
\tilde{\omega}
) must positively intersect one of the divisor components
D_{\mathsf{q}}
with
\mathsf{q}\neq\mathsf{p}
.
It follows from this that all of the classes
H_{log}^{*\oplus\mathsf{p}}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
are tautologically admissible in the sense of
[
GP21
, §3.3]
and there is therefore a log PSS map:

\displaystyle PSS^{log}:H_{log}^{*\oplus\mathsf{p}}(X_{t,\kappa},D)\to SH^{*\oplus\mathsf{p}}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0}).

(6.11)

Note that the classical PSS construction defines a map:

\displaystyle PSS:H^{*\oplus 0}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)\to SH^{*\oplus 0}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0}).

(6.12)

Proposition 55
.

Given
\alpha_{1}\in H^{*\oplus 0}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
and
\alpha_{2}\in H^{*\oplus\mathsf{p}}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D),

\displaystyle PSS(\alpha_{1})\ast PSS^{log}(\alpha_{2})=PSS^{log}(\alpha_{1}\ast_{log}\alpha_{2}).

(6.13)

Proof 6.5
.

This follows from the argument of
[
GP20
, Theorem 5.12]
. That argument interpolates between the two operations using a certain moduli space of thimbles with two marked points and it suffices to show that no sphere bubbling can arise in the interpolating moduli space.
Suppose, to the contrary, that such a non-constant sphere bubble arose.
In the present case, the thimbles intersect
D_{\mathsf{p}}
and no other divisor component.
By Corollary
54
, the union of all the sphere bubbles must positively intersect one of the divisors
D_{\mathsf{q}},\mathsf{q}\neq\mathsf{p}.

This would mean the thimble obtained by removing all of the sphere bubbles would have negative intersection with this divisor
D_{\mathsf{q}}
, but the thimbles intersect each component of
D
non-negatively by positivity of intersection, a contradiction.

Proposition 56
.

We have:

(i)

the map
PSS^{log}:H^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0};\Bbbk)\to SH^{2\oplus 0}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0};\Bbbk)
is surjective.

(ii)

if
\mathsf{p}\in P
, then
SH^{2\oplus\mathsf{p}}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0};\Bbbk)
has rank one and is generated by the class
PSS^{log}(\gamma_{\mathsf{p}})
, where
\gamma_{\mathsf{p}}
is the class defined in
Lemma

52
.

(iii)

SH^{2\oplus\mathsf{p}}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0};\Bbbk)=0
unless
\mathsf{p}\in P\cup\{0\}
.

Proof 6.6
.

By
[
GP20
, Theorem 1.1]
, there is a convergent spectral sequence

\displaystyle E_{r}^{\ast-q,q}\Rightarrow SH^{*}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0})

(6.14)

and an isomorphism

\displaystyle H^{*}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)\cong\bigoplus_{q}E_{1}^{\ast-q,q}.

(6.15)

The spectral sequence is also compatible with
M^{*}
- gradings. The higher differentials in the spectral sequence, when applied to a log cohomology class
\alpha\mathbf{t}^{\mathsf{v}}
, only involve terms
\tilde{\alpha}\mathbf{t}^{\tilde{\mathsf{v}}}
with
\tilde{\mathsf{v}}\neq\mathsf{v}
. It therefore follows from Lemma
51
that the spectral sequence degenerates at the
E_{1}
page. With this in place, the proofs of these claims are as follows:

Item

(i)
follows from the fact that on
H^{*\oplus 0}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
, which is isomorphic to
H^{*}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0})
by Lemma
52
, (
6.15
) is the associated graded of the PSS map (
6.12
).

To prove
Item

(ii)
, we first note that by combining Lemma
52
with the collapse of the spectral sequence, we have that
SH^{2\oplus\mathsf{p}}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0};\Bbbk)\cong H^{0}(SD_{\mathsf{p}})
, which implies that it has rank one. The fact that
PSS^{log}(\gamma_{\mathsf{p}})
is a generator for
SH^{2\oplus\mathsf{p}}(\mathcal{H}_{t,0};\Bbbk)
follows from the fact that, on
H^{*\oplus\mathsf{p}}_{log}(X_{t,\kappa},D)
, (
6.15
) is the associated graded of the log PSS map (
6.11
).

Item

(iii)
is immediate from
Item

(iii)
of Lemma
52
